		<item>
		<title>Evolving from legacy BI to agentic AI at Tradeshift with Amazon Quick</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/evolving-from-legacy-bi-to-agentic-ai-at-tradeshift-with-amazon-quick/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Raphael Bres]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Mon, 20 Jul 2026 16:56:48 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Amazon Quick Suite]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Customer Solutions]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">9b1c53492191b8a5a6b9ba7b92b06bab8b7e6f1d</guid>

					<description>In this post, we describe how Tradeshift deployed Amazon Quick with agentic AI capabilities to replace our legacy BI tool, resulting in query response times up to 30 times faster, a 40 percent reduction in total cost of ownership, and turned embedded analytics into a product that generates revenue.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;This guest post is co-written by Raphael Bres, Robert Iordache, Anca Andone, Ioana Millon (Ploesteanu) of Tradeshift and Roy Yung of AWS&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span class="c-mrkdwn__draggable-link"&gt;&lt;a class="c-link" href="https://tradeshift.com/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" data-stringify-link="https://tradeshift.com/" data-sk="tooltip_parent"&gt;Tradeshift&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/span&gt; is an AI-powered accounts payable (AP) and e-Invoicing compliance platform serving buyers and sellers in more than 70 countries, with a cloud-based network that processes millions of transactions. We serve both sides of a marketplace, buyers and sellers, so our analytics needs are different from most companies. When our in-house business intelligence (BI) tool couldn’t handle our growing data volumes and customer expectations, we switched to &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/quicksuite/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Quick&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Amazon Quick is an agentic AI workspace that connects to all your applications, tools, and data. It includes multiple features such as chat agent for natural language Q&amp;amp;A over your business data, Flows for automating multi-step workflows without coding, and Research for producing comprehensive, long-form analytical reports from multiple sources. These capabilities are built on AWS with enterprise-level security.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In this post, we describe how Tradeshift deployed Amazon Quick with agentic AI capabilities to replace our legacy BI tool, resulting in query response times up to 30 times faster, a 40 percent reduction in total cost of ownership, and turned embedded analytics into a product that generates revenue.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="limitations-of-our-legacy-analytics-tool"&gt;Limitations of our legacy analytics tool&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For several years, Tradeshift relied on a proprietary BI tool built in-house. While it served basic reporting needs in the early stages of its growth, the tool imposed constraints that became untenable as our data volumes and customer expectations increased. Maintaining the tool consumed approximately 50 percent of one full-time employee’s capacity, diverting engineering resources away from product innovation. The tool supported a maximum of 10,000 rows per query, imposed a 25 MB ceiling on scheduled reports, and retained only six months of historical data. With these constraints, large-scale trend analysis, anomaly detection, or predictive modeling couldn’t be performed.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The team sought a platform that would eliminate heavy maintenance, deliver enterprise-level performance for big data workloads, allow users to access and interact with data directly, and offer AI-powered natural language querying to democratize access to insights. Tradeshift’s Accounts Payable and Finance users needed heavy statistical analysis and data crunching at their fingertips, decoupled from the technical skills traditionally required for this kind of data introspection.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Beyond technical constraints, the company’s Customer Success, Data and Analytics, and Commercial teams spent hours weekly on manual reporting such as exporting CSVs, running Excel macros, and assembling reports by hand. External customers who needed deeper insights into their AP workflows had no self-service path. They depended on Tradeshift’s engineering and BI teams to produce custom reports, creating bottlenecks that slowed decision-making on both sides.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;These challenges pointed to two categories of solutions that only agentic AI could address. First, automated workflows were needed to schedule dataset refreshes, trigger report distribution, and orchestrate recurring analytics tasks without manual intervention. The company has delivered these capabilities through Quick’s AI-powered workflow engine &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/quick/flows/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Quick Flows&lt;/a&gt; and deep research engine &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/quick/research/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Quick Research&lt;/a&gt;. Second, a conversational chat interface was required to interpret natural language queries and instantly retrieve document metrics and operational data, eliminating the dependency on BI teams for day-to-day questions. This was achieved by building the AP Auditor chat agent powered by &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/quick/chat-agents/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Quick chat agent&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The team evaluated several alternatives, but Quick was the only solution offering scalability for large datasets, multi-device embedded access, AI-powered natural language querying, workflow, and unified internal and customer-facing analytics. Its agentic capabilities, including what-if modeling, automated workflows, deep research, and a conversational chat agent, together democratize data access across the organization.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="building-an-embedded-analytics-product-on-quick"&gt;Building an embedded analytics product on Quick&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Tradeshift’s implementation followed a phased approach. The team began with a proof of concept in early 2024, progressed through an embedded analytics minimum viable product (MVP) from August 2024 through March 2025, and launched the full Reporting and Analytics app for buyers and sellers in June 2025. By August 2025, Quick had also replaced its internal BI tooling, achieving 98 percent adoption across the organization.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The architecture serves three layers of analytics capability. The first layer consists of 16 embedded &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/quick/quicksight/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Quick Sight&lt;/a&gt; BI dashboards delivered through secure iFrames, covering nine domains: document invoicing, purchase orders, scanning, network connections, user activity, workflow automation, compliance and anomaly detection, payment prediction, and goods receipt-invoice reconciliation. These dashboards process between one million and one hundred million transaction records and return results in under three seconds, compared to the 45 to 90 seconds the legacy tool required.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The second layer provides conversational AI access to the same underlying data. Users can ask natural language questions without needing SQL knowledge or data analytics expertise, and receive instant visual responses. The third layer introduces tiered data autonomy: a Standard tier offering pre-built dashboards with basic filters and CSV export, and a Premium tier that includes Designer Mode, custom dashboard creation, What-If modeling scenarios, and access to agentic AI capabilities.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Security runs through four layers. Okta single sign-on (SSO) handles user authentication. &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quick/latest/userguide/namespaces.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Quick custom namespaces&lt;/a&gt; isolate each tenant. Signed URLs provide time-limited, session-bound embedding. And &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quick/latest/userguide/restrict-access-to-a-data-set-using-row-level-security.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;row-level security&lt;/a&gt; (about 14,000 RLS rules) filters data to each user’s context. To learn more about multi-tenant embedded dashboard, see &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/business-intelligence/support-multi-tenant-applications-for-saas-environments-using-amazon-quicksight/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Support multi-tenant applications for SaaS environments using Amazon Quick Sight&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following diagram illustrates Tradeshift’s embedded Quick Sight dashboard architecture.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img loading="lazy" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-135550" src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/14/Analytics-Infrastructure-Diagram-Copy-of-WhiteBackground-2.png" alt="" width="3994" height="3019"&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="measurable-business-results"&gt;Measurable business results&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With the implementation of Amazon Quick, Tradeshift observed significant improvements in operational efficiency. Its internal accounts and support teams save 8.5 hours per week that were previously spent on manual CSV reporting. External buyers save 6 to 8 hours per week per user. Manual data manipulation, including Excel macros, VLOOKUPs, and pivot table assembly, decreased by 80 percent. The time required to identify operational bottlenecks dropped from one to two days to under five seconds.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;From a cost perspective, the company achieved a 40 percent reduction in total cost of ownership compared to its previous solution, a 35 percent reduction in infrastructure costs by shifting queries off production databases to &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quick/latest/userguide/spice.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;SPICE&lt;/a&gt;, Quick Sight’s in-memory data engine, and a 30 percent consolidation of licensing costs across previously fragmented BI tools. Routine maintenance now requires only 0.5 full-time equivalent (FTE), down from the 50 percent FTE burden of the legacy system.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The company realized a 2 percent incremental annual recurring revenue (ARR) expansion from existing buyer accounts through our premium reporting tier. Time-to-market for custom enterprise reporting improved by 75 percent. New reports that previously took four to six weeks now deploy in approximately one week. Accounts using embedded analytics show a 10 percent higher retention rate over 12 months compared to those that do not.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Tradeshift reached 50 percent active monthly adoption across targeted enterprise buyers within the first year, achieved a 2x increase in analytics utilization by non-technical staff, and saw an 80 percent reduction in analytics-related support tickets. Internally, 98 percent of the organization actively uses the platform.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="solving-real-customer-problems-with-amazon-quick"&gt;Solving real customer problems with Amazon Quick&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following two use cases highlight how Quick’s AI and BI capabilities address everyday business challenges for Tradeshift’s customers.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The first use case centers on a generative AI-powered AP Auditor chat agent, built using Amazon Quick’s &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quick/latest/userguide/custom-agents.html#create-custom-agents" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;custom chat agent&lt;/a&gt;. Previously, AP auditors lacked immediate self-service access to document data. Resolving routine queries required manual effort and cross-functional dependencies on internal BI or engineering teams. This created bottlenecks that delayed real-time decision-making. With Amazon Quick, the team defined a purpose-built agent for the AP auditor persona using natural language instructions alone, without writing code. Through &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/quick/spaces/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Quick Spaces,&lt;/a&gt; administrators control exactly which data sources the agent can access, ensuring every response is grounded in permissioned data. Users can now ask &lt;em&gt;“Show me pending invoices from last month”&lt;/em&gt; or &lt;em&gt;“What’s the status of PO #12345?”&lt;/em&gt; and receive immediate, accurate answers without waiting on other teams.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Behind the agent sits a comprehensive knowledge architecture. The AP Auditor connects to a dedicated &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quick/latest/userguide/knowledge-base-integrations.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Quick Knowledge bases&lt;/a&gt; containing 11 reference documents spanning platform capabilities, policies, and process guides, alongside 9 dashboards, 14 curated query topics, and 68 automated action tools accessible via the Model Context Protocol (MCP). With this architecture, the agent goes beyond simple data retrieval. It can explain metric definitions, locate source data, and provide business context that previously required consulting a subject matter expert.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following video demonstrates Tradeshift’s generative AI-powered AP Auditor chat agent&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 640px;" class="wp-video"&gt;
 &lt;video class="wp-video-shortcode" id="video-135398-1" width="640" height="360" preload="metadata" controls="controls"&gt;
  &lt;source type="video/mp4" src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/artifacts/DBSBlogs/ML-21151/ML-21151-video1.mp4?_=1"&gt;
 &lt;/video&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The second use case demonstrates Amazon Quick’s ability to handle high-volume BI workloads with fast query performance. A client lacked visibility into the manual operational overhead. Their AP team needed to quantify human effort required, understand workload distribution, and measure total approval workflow overhead. Using Amazon Quick data cache layer SPICE, the team built a reporting solution that processes large volumes of line-level coding data at speed. The solution distinguishes manual corrections from AI-generated codes, identifies individual coders, and tracks the full approval hierarchy. The client now has near real-time and historical visibility into operations, enabling them to spot bottlenecks. The same datasets and dashboards can also serve as knowledge sources for custom chat agents, extending BI insights into conversational AI.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="transforming-internal-operations"&gt;Transforming internal operations&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Internally, Tradeshift migrated from its legacy BI tool to Quick Sight and collapsed 40 separate dashboards that users previously had to navigate, into more than 100 queryable datasets accessible through natural language. Its teams in Finance, Operations, Product, and Customer Success can now ask questions in plain language and get answers immediately, with no SQL or analyst required.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The quarterly market analysis process previously required multiple weeks of manual data gathering, cross-referencing, and interpretation. It now completes in days or hours using Quick Research, which generates multi-source analysis and synthesized reports from a single prompt. Rather than starting from scratch, analysts build on these research artifacts to conduct further deep-dive analytics, compressing what once took days into hours. Using Quick’s chat agent, they can interrogate data directly, generate visualizations, and produce executive summaries ready to share with stakeholders.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Recurring reporting now flows through automated Quick Flows, a no-code automation engine. Teams use it to orchestrate dataset refreshes, report generation, and distribution as scheduled, repeatable pipelines. More than 270 SPICE datasets refresh daily, while reports are distributed automatically on daily, weekly, and monthly cadences without manual intervention. This freed the analytics team to focus on higher-value strategic work rather than routine data pipeline maintenance.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following video shows how Tradeshift uses Amazon Quick Flows and Research to automate its quarterly market analysis&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 640px;" class="wp-video"&gt;
 &lt;video class="wp-video-shortcode" id="video-135398-2" width="640" height="360" preload="metadata" controls="controls"&gt;
  &lt;source type="video/mp4" src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/artifacts/DBSBlogs/ML-21151/ML-21151-video2.mp4?_=2"&gt;
 &lt;/video&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="what-makes-this-approach-different"&gt;What makes this approach different&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Tradeshift’s customers are Accounts Payable teams, finance professionals managing invoices, approvals, and payment cycles across thousands of suppliers. These users are not data analysts. Before the company embedded agentic AI into its analytics platform, getting answers from their own transaction data meant filing a ticket, waiting on a BI team, and receiving a static report days or weeks later.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;That is the experience most AP automation platforms still deliver today. Tradeshift chose a different path. In 2025, it became the first in the AP automation and e-invoicing market to embed Amazon Quick for natural language querying, letting customers ask plain-language questions of their own data and receive instant responses without SQL or schema knowledge. In 2026, the company was a pioneer to integrate their MCP server with Amazon Quick, so that generative AI agents can read data catalog metadata, interpret Quick Sight schemas, and produce written insights autonomously.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For its customers, this pace of innovation is the difference between self-service and dependency. Because the company moves fast and collaborates closely with AWS, every new agentic capability ships to the customer base within days or weeks. Custom development would have taken months or years. Users receive answers grounded in metric definitions from its knowledge bases, complete with explanations of how each number is calculated and where the source data lives. This eliminates cross-functional dependencies on engineering and BI teams entirely, collapsing what used to be multi-day report cycles into seconds.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This positions Tradeshift customers at the forefront of AP analytics innovation. They engage with their data conversationally by asking questions, generating insights, and acting on real-time information in ways that static dashboards cannot deliver.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="future-developments"&gt;Future developments&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Tradeshift’s roadmap focuses on three priorities. First, the team plans to enable write access on its production MCP server. Agents can then take live action on behalf of users, not just read and analyze data. Second, the company intends to embed a generic chat agent into the Standard tier of the analytics application, giving every user access to agentic AI by default, not just Premium subscribers. Third, it is expanding the &lt;a href="https://tradeshift.com/resources/ai/analytics-new-reports-dashboards-for-sellers/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Reporting and Analytics application to seller&lt;/a&gt; users across our network, extending its value to both sides of the marketplace.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/07/13/ML-21151-4.png" alt="Timeline of Tradeshift’s journey from legacy BI to agentic AI with Amazon Quick, from 2023 discovery through analytics milestones to agentic AI in 2027" width="800"&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="next-steps"&gt;Next steps&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Amazon Quick and agentic AI helped Tradeshift transform analytics from a cost center into a revenue-generating product. By replacing a constrained legacy tool with a scalable, AI-powered platform, we reduced query times by 30 times, cut total cost of ownership by 40 percent, and delivered a self-service analytics experience that customers and internal teams actively choose to use every day. For organizations managing complex AP workflows at scale, the ability to ask questions in natural language and receive immediate, accurate, contextual answers means teams spend less time on manual reporting and more time acting on insight.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To get started with your own analytics solution, explore the following resources:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Transform your AP data with &lt;a href="https://tradeshift.com/analytics-app/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Tradeshift’s AI-powered Reporting and Analytics app&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Learn more about Quick analytics capabilities in the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quicksuite/latest/userguide/welcome.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Quick User Guide&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Join the discussion in the &lt;a href="https://community.amazonquicksight.com/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Quick Community&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;

					<description><![CDATA[<p>AT&#38;T processed approximately one trillion tokens while developing OTel2.0 using Microsoft Foundry Managed Compute, open AI models, and AMD and NVIDIA GPU infrastructure. Discover how flexible model choice and scalable infrastructure are enabling production-scale telecom AI.</p>
<p>The post <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/att-and-microsoft-scale-trillion-token-workloads-with-microsoft-foundry-and-amd/">AT&amp;T and Microsoft scale trillion-token workloads with Microsoft Foundry and AMD</a> appeared first on <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog">Microsoft Azure Blog</a>.</p>
]]></description>
										<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="first-of-its-kind-telecom-ai-deployment">First-of-its-kind telecom AI deployment</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Telecommunications organizations are increasingly looking to AI to help teams navigate highly specialized domains, but generic models often lack the industry-specific knowledge needed to understand telecom networks, standards, and operations. To address that gap, AT&amp;T created their Open Telco (OTel) models, the next generation of telecom-focused AI designed to bring deeper telecommunications expertise into AI systems. Building OTel2.0 required more than training a large language model, it reflected a broader issue many organizations face: how to build domain-specific AI systems at scale while balancing cost, performance, and operational complexity. Cost management quickly became a key consideration. To continue advancing telecom-focused AI, AT&amp;T needed a platform capable of supporting OTel2.0 development at an entirely new scale.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Where teams previously had to own and manage deployments, infrastructure, and the associated operational overhead, <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/concepts/managed-compute-overview">Foundry Managed Compute</a> provided a more streamlined way to access dedicated graphics processing unit (GPU) capacity. This transformation requires more than powerful models; it requires the ability to scale without compromising cost, flexibility, or performance.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://www.gsma.com/newsroom/press-release/gsma-launches-open-telco-ai-to-accelerate-development-of-telco%E2%80%91grade-ai/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer noreferrer noopener">Learn how OTel2.0 scales telecom AI<strong style="font-family: inherit;font-size: 15.008px;font-style: inherit;letter-spacing: 0.30016px;text-transform: inherit"></strong></a></div>
</div>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Using <a href="https://devblogs.microsoft.com/foundry/announcing-foundry-managed-compute/">Microsoft Foundry Managed Compute</a>, AT&amp;T was able to experiment across multiple open models, optimize workloads across different GPU architectures, and process massive volumes of telecom data all within a unified platform. The result was an AI development environment capable of supporting trillions of tokens while giving teams the flexibility to iterate, optimize, and innovate faster.
</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="model-choice-meets-infrastructure-flexibility">Model choice meets infrastructure flexibility </h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Building OTel2.0 required flexibility across both models and infrastructure. Rather than standardizing on a single model, AT&amp;T adopted a multi open-model strategy. Open models were central to AT&amp;T’s approach because they provided the flexibility to work with approved telecom data, tailor the workflow for domain-specific model development, and support large-scale experimentation with greater control over cost and deployment strategy. Through <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/ai-foundry" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Foundry</a>, the team deployed several models from the Hugging Face collection, including <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/phi" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Phi-4</a>, OSS-120B, and Gemma-4, to support different stages of development, from synthetic data generation and data preparation to reasoning-intensive workloads and broader model development efforts. Phi-4 played a significant role in this process, processing more than 700 billion tokens a month as part of the broader data preparation and training workflow for OTel2.0.</p>



<blockquote class="wp-block-quote has-quote-default-font-size is-layout-flow wp-block-quote-is-layout-flow">
<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Every company in the world needs to build its own AI, and that is only possible with open models and open source. AT&amp;T is championing this vision, building on open models like Phi-4 and Gemma, and giving OTel back to the community as a telecom AI foundation others can build upon. Microsoft Foundry makes this practical at scale, bringing the latest open models from the Hugging Face collection together with AMD and NVIDIA GPUs in one place, so teams can pick the right model and the right hardware, then deploy in hours instead of weeks.</p>
<cite>—Jeff Boudier, Vice President of Product, Hugging Face</cite></blockquote>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://www.gsma.com/newsroom/press-release/gsma-launches-open-telco-ai-to-accelerate-development-of-telco%E2%80%91grade-ai/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Developing OTel2.0</a> also required infrastructure capable of operating at telecom scale. AT&amp;T used approximately <strong>530 GPUs</strong> through Microsoft Foundry Managed Compute spanning multiple GPU architectures including <strong>430 AMD Instinct™ MI300X GPUs</strong>. This heterogenous approach gave AT&amp;T more flexibility in how models were deployed and optimized as requirements evolved.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-table aligncenter is-style-stripes"><table class="has-white-color has-electric-blue-400-background-color has-text-color has-background has-link-color has-fixed-layout"><thead><tr><th class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">Model</th><th class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">Example workload</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">Phi-4</td><td class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">Around 700B tokens a month for <br>data preparation and synthetic <br>data generation</td></tr><tr><td class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">OSS 120B</td><td class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">Higher-reasoning workloads </td></tr><tr><td class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">Gemma 4</td><td class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">OTel2.0 development workflows</td></tr></tbody></table><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Table 1:&nbsp;Explains what&nbsp;open source&nbsp;models were used and how</figcaption></figure>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">This flexibility illustrates a broader trend across AI development. Organizations increasingly need platforms that allow them to choose the right model for the job, optimize for cost and performance, and scale workloads without rebuilding operational environments. <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/ai-foundry" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Foundry</a> brings model choice, infrastructure flexibility, governance, and operational scale together in a unified platform that supports those requirements.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/ai-foundry" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Start building with Microsoft Foundry</a></div>
</div>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Beyond flexibility and cost, deployment speed is a critical factor for many AI initiatives. As workloads expand and new models are evaluated, the ability to access GPU capacity quickly enables teams to move from experimentation to execution faster without lengthy provisioning cycles. With Foundry Managed Compute, AT&amp;T could deploy and scale models in days rather than waiting weeks for infrastructure to become available, helping accelerate development timelines and maintain momentum across OTel2.0 development.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="optimizing-cost-without-limiting-innovation">Optimizing cost without limiting innovation  </h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  As AI workloads grow, economics become as important as model performance. For AT&amp;T,  one of the primary objectives was to lower AI model consumption costs while continuing to drive meaningful business value through AI-powered innovation. By using open models on <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/concepts/managed-compute-overview">Microsoft Foundry Managed Compute</a>, AT&amp;T was able to support large-scale data preparation and model development using a different economic model built around dedicated GPU infrastructure and open-model flexibility.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The impact became clear at scale. In support of OTel2.0, AT&amp;T processed approximately 1T tokens, consisting of raw documents from GSMA supplemented by synthetic data generated. Generating the data using open-source models like Phi-4, served by Microsoft&#8217;s Foundry Managed Compute, saved tens of millions of dollars versus using frontier models. This allowed teams to invest in larger-scale experimentation and development while maintaining a focus on business value and operational efficiency.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-table aligncenter is-style-stripes has-body-medium-font-size"><table class="has-white-color has-electric-blue-400-background-color has-text-color has-background has-link-color has-fixed-layout"><thead><tr><th>Metric</th><th class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">Value</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>OTel 1.0 Downloads</td><td class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">Over 25M</td></tr><tr><td>GPUs Used Through Foundry Managed Compute</td><td class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">About 530</td></tr><tr><td>Tokens Processed for OTel2.0</td><td class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">About 1T</td></tr><tr><td>Tokens Trained for OTel2.0</td><td class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">About&nbsp;400&nbsp;B</td></tr><tr><td>Models used to train OTel</td><td class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">Phi-4, OSS 120B, Gemma 4</td></tr></tbody></table><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Table 2: Quick facts about the OTel model family and metrics around what was used to build OTel2.0&nbsp;</figcaption></figure>



<blockquote class="wp-block-quote has-quote-default-font-size is-layout-flow wp-block-quote-is-layout-flow">
<p class="wp-block-paragraph">When you are processing hundreds of billions of tokens, infrastructure becomes part of the problem you solve. Foundry Managed Compute gave us access to GPU capacity at scale so our teams could focus on advancing OTel2.0 instead of managing infrastructure.</p>
<cite>—Mark Austin, Vice President, Data Science and AI at AT&amp;T</cite></blockquote>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  At this scale, infrastructure is no longer simply a <a id="post-52857-_Int_JNQm0EQ5"></a>deployment consideration. It <a id="post-52857-_Int_PdbJnM1r"></a>becomes a strategic component of AI development.
</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="accelerating-the-next-wave-of-production-scale-ai">Accelerating the next wave of production-scale AI</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">OTel 2.0 demonstrates how organizations can combine open models, scalable infrastructure, and domain expertise to build production-ready AI systems. By matching different models to different workloads and optimizing infrastructure for cost and performance, AT&amp;T was able to process trillions of tokens while maintaining operational efficiency.&nbsp;</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">As organizations move from AI experimentation to production deployment, they increasingly need the flexibility to choose the right models, optimize infrastructure, and scale efficiently. <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/ai-foundry" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Foundry</a> and <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/concepts/managed-compute-overview" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Foundry Managed Compute</a> help support that transition by bringing those capabilities together in a unified platform.</p>

					<description><![CDATA[<p>Microsoft Azure Databricks delivers the first-party advantage of Databricks on Microsoft—and for customers, that advantage shows up as real, measurable value. It is the same Databricks platform your teams already know, co-engineered with Microsoft and delivered as a native Azure service, so it fits naturally into the Microsoft tools, identity, and governance your organization already runs.</p>
<p>The post <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/azure-databricks-delivers-proven-business-value/">Azure Databricks delivers proven business value</a> appeared first on <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog">Microsoft Azure Blog</a>.</p>
]]></description>
										<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<p class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/databricks/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Azure Databricks</a> delivers the first-party advantage of Databricks on Microsoft—and for customers, that advantage shows up as real, measurable value. It is the same Databricks platform your teams already know, co-engineered with Microsoft and delivered as a native <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure service</a>, so it fits naturally into the Microsoft tools, identity, and governance your organization already runs.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/databricks/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Discover Azure Databricks</a></div>
</div>



<p class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-paragraph">
  The advantage is built-in, not bolted on. Microsoft and Databricks co-engineer the service, share one integration roadmap across the Microsoft data and AI stack, and align go-to-market so you get one motion, one bill, and one support path. For technical teams, that means deeper native integration and stronger performance. For the business, it means lower cost, less risk, and faster time to value.
</p>



<p class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-paragraph">The strategic partnership drives an accelerated integration roadmap and continuous optimization for improved performance; however, decision-makers constantly ask about what business value all of this translates to. To address this key question, Microsoft commissioned a <a href="https://tei.forrester.com/go/Microsoft/Databricks/?lang=en-us" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Forrester Total Economic Impact™ study</a> of <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/databricks/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Azure Databricks</a>. It found that a composite organization based on interviewed customers realized a three-year <strong>331% return on investment</strong>, <strong>$58.1 million in net present value</strong>, and recovered its investment in <strong>less than six months</strong>.</p>



<div class="section wp-block-bloginabox-theme-section has-global-padding is-layout-constrained wp-block-bloginabox-theme-section-is-layout-constrained">
	
<div
	class="section__background alignfull is-layout-constrained"
	>

				<div class="section-background-image-wrapper section-background-image-wrapper--light section-background-image-wrapper--desktop alignfull section--emerald-dusk" style="opacity: 100%">
					</div>
	
				<div class="section-background-image-wrapper section-background-image-wrapper--dark section-background-image-wrapper--desktop alignfull" style="opacity: 100%">
					</div>
	
		
		</div>
<div class="section__content alignfull is-layout-constrained">
	

<figure class="wp-block-table aligncenter has-body-large-font-size"><table class="has-white-color has-electric-blue-200-background-color has-text-color has-background has-link-color has-fixed-layout"><tbody><tr><td class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center"><strong>331%</strong><br>Return on investment&nbsp;</td><td class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center"><strong>$58.1M</strong><br>Net present value</td><td class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center"><strong>&lt; 6 months</strong><br>Payback period</td></tr></tbody></table></figure>

</div>
</div>



<p class="has-text-align-center has-body-medium-font-size wp-block-paragraph"><em>Commissioned study conducted by Forrester Consulting on behalf of Microsoft, June 2026. Results are over three years and represent a composite organization based on interviewed customers and may not be typical; actual results will vary.</em>
</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="what-the-study-found">What the study found</h2>



<p class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-paragraph">Forrester interviewed <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/databricks/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Azure Databricks</a> customers and built a composite organization to model the impact: a $6 billion company in a regulated industry, running about 10 petabytes of data.</p>



<p class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-paragraph">
  Before Azure Databricks, its data estate was fragmented and expensive. It was unreliable at scale and hard to govern. Afterward, the results were clear: $75.6 million in benefits against $17.5 million in cost over three years translating to $58.1 million in net present value.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  The value came from four places:
</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-list-item"><strong>$39.0 million—data and analytics teams’ productivity</strong>. Teams handled more work without adding people, with measured gains of 15% to 25%. As a Vice President of data services at a healthcare organization put it: “<em>…we’re doing more work with the same size of the team</em>.”</li>



<li class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-list-item"><strong>$19.9 million—lower infrastructure costs</strong>. Elastic, pay-as-you-go compute replaced overprovisioned hardware.</li>



<li class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-list-item"><strong>$11.4 million—better data platform resiliency</strong>. Managed operations meant fewer outages and no custom disaster recovery to build.</li>



<li class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-list-item"><strong>$5.4 million—retired legacy software and redeployed DBAs</strong>.<strong>&nbsp;</strong>Consolidating databases and Extract, Load, Transform (ETL) tools eliminated third-party licenses, and managed operations freed database administrators for higher-value work.</li>
</ul>



<p class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-paragraph">Forrester listed more benefits it didn’t put a price on: native Azure services integration, faster insights, wider access to data, and governance through Unity Catalog. That’s where the return starts.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://tei.forrester.com/go/Microsoft/Databricks" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Model your own numbers with the Azure Databricks ROI estimator</a></div>
</div>



<figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-large"><a href="https://tei.forrester.com/go/Microsoft/Databricks/?lang=en-us" target="_blank" rel=" noreferrer noopener"><img fetchpriority="high" decoding="async" width="791" height="1024" src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/TEI-of-ADB-infographic-791x1024.jpg" alt="The Total Economic Impact of Microsoft Azure Databricks infographic" class="wp-image-52760" srcset="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/TEI-of-ADB-infographic-791x1024.jpg 791w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/TEI-of-ADB-infographic-232x300.jpg 232w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/TEI-of-ADB-infographic-768x994.jpg 768w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/TEI-of-ADB-infographic-1187x1536.jpg 1187w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/TEI-of-ADB-infographic-1583x2048.jpg 1583w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/TEI-of-ADB-infographic.jpg 1700w" sizes="(max-width: 791px) 100vw, 791px" /></a></figure>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="where-the-value-comes-from">Where the value comes from</h2>



<p class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-paragraph">Those returns come down to one thing. <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/databricks/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Azure Databricks</a> is a true first-party Azure service, co-engineered by Microsoft and Databricks, it plugs into the tools your teams already use. That removes the extra data copies, tooling, and integration work that raise costs elsewhere.</p>



<p class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-paragraph">A great example is the <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/databricks/genie-one/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure Databricks Genie</a> integration with <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-365-copilot/cowork" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Copilot Cowork</a>. You can add context of your business and build on that intelligence into the tools your teams already use with this integration. <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/databricks/genie-ui/genie" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Genie</a> lets anyone question the lakehouse in plain language—now inside <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/databricks/integrations/msft-teams" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Teams</a>, <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/databricks/integrations/msft-m365-copilot" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft 365 Copilot</a>, and more recently in <a href="https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/azure-databricks/azure-databricks-at-databricks-data--ai-summit-2026-updates-and-new-announcement/4528388" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Copilot Cowork</a>, where it grounds tasks in trusted data through Genie Ontology. Every answer is scoped by Unity Catalog to exactly what each user is permitted to see, so intelligence reaches the flow of work without loosening governance.</p>



<p class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-paragraph">
  The same depth runs across the rest of the platform:
</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-list-item"><strong>Identity and governance</strong>: <a href="https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/azure-databricks/general-availability-automatic-identity-management-aim-for-entra-id-on-azure-dat/4452206" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Automatic Identity Management for Entra ID</a> syncs users into <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/databricks/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Azure Databricks</a>. Unity Catalog and <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-ie/security/business/microsoft-purview" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Microsoft Purview</a> govern the rest.&nbsp;</li>



<li class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-list-item"><strong>Microsoft Power BI and Microsoft 365</strong>: <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-ie/power-platform/products/power-bi">Power BI</a> reads your data directly and now <a href="https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/azure-databricks/closing-the-loop-interactive-write-back-from-power-bi-to-azure-databricks/4442999">writes back to it</a>. A new Excel add-in brings governed data into spreadsheets; a SharePoint connector streams files into Delta tables, and <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/databricks/ai-bi/admin/teams-subscriptions">Teams notifications</a> deliver alerts where teams work. <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/databricks/metric-views/">Metric views</a> keep business logic consistent across all of them.&nbsp;</li>



<li class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-list-item"><strong>AI and agents</strong>: Beyond chatting with your data, <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/databricks/genie-ui/genie" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Genie</a> connects to <a href="https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/azure-databricks/azure-databricks-genie-integration-with-copilot-studio-and-microsoft-foundry-is-/4471087" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Copilot Studio and Microsoft Foundry</a>, and a single Model Context Protocol (MCP) connection lets <a href="https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/analyticsonazure/building-ai-apps-and-agents-with-azure-databricks-copilot-studio-and-github-copi/4524065" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Copilot Studio and GitHub Copilot agents</a> reason over an entire Azure Databricks workspace. <a href="https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/azure-databricks/azure-databricks-lakebase-is-now-generally-available/4498779" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Azure Database Lakebase</a> gives agents a serverless Postgres engine, and <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/databricks/admin/workspace/serverless-workspaces" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">serverless workspaces</a> get them started fast.&nbsp;</li>



<li class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-list-item"><strong>Microsoft OneLake</strong>: <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/databricks/query-federation/onelake" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">OneLake catalog federation</a> lets Azure Databricks query OneLake data directly, with no pipelines or copies. You can also <a href="https://community.fabric.microsoft.com/t5/Fabric-Updates-Blog/Extending-interoperability-Azure-Databricks-can-now-store-Unity/ba-p/5199741" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">store Unity Catalog tables in OneLake</a>, alongside Azure Data Lake Storage.</li>



<li class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-list-item"><strong>Customer data</strong>: <a href="https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/azure-databricks/azure-databricks-at-databricks-data--ai-summit-2026-updates-and-new-announcement/4528388">CustomerLake</a>, a new Agentic Customer Data platform, builds Customer 360 profiles and runs campaigns inside the lakehouse where the data and governance already live.&nbsp;</li>



<li class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-list-item"><strong>Enterprise systems</strong>: <a href="https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/azure-databricks/sap-business-data-cloud-connect-with-azure-databricks-is-now-generally-available/4459490">SAP Business Data Cloud Connect</a> brings SAP data into the lakehouse. It’s the same model helping industries like <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-cloud/blog/telecommunications/2026/02/24/microsoft-accelerates-telecom-return-on-intelligence-with-a-unified-trusted-ai-platform/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">industries like telecom</a> turn AI into returns.&nbsp;</li>
</ul>



<p class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-paragraph">
  These are the integrations that Forrester valued but didn’t price separately however, they are critical factors driving productivity and cost benefits that were quantified.
</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="backed-by-independent-benchmarks">Backed by independent benchmarks</h2>



<p class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-paragraph">Value also depends on speed, and that’s been tested. Principled Technologies, an independent firm, ran an industry-standard, TPC-DS-like <a href="https://www.principledtechnologies.com/clients/reports/Microsoft/Azure-Databricks-competitive-0725/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">decision-support benchmark</a> on a 10-terabyte dataset. <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/databricks/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Azure Databricks</a> completed a single query stream in up to 21.1% less time than Databricks on AWS (with autoscale disabled) and ran four concurrent query streams more than nine minutes faster.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="what-it-means-for-you">What it means for you</h2>



<p class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-paragraph">Choosing a data and AI platform is a long-term decision, and with <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/databricks/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Azure Databricks</a> the pieces reinforce each other. The integration drives the savings Forrester measured. The performance keeps those gains steady as usage grows. And it all rests on one foundation: a first-party partnership that puts Microsoft and Databricks engineering, roadmap, and support behind your data estate. The value isn’t a claim, it’s been measured: a three-year 331% return, with payback in under six months. It’s why so many teams choose to run their lakehouse on Azure Databricks.</p>

<div class="wp-block-bloginabox-theme-post-alert" role="alert" aria-live="polite" data-bi-an="alert">
	<div class="alert-content">
		<strong>What&#8217;s new today</strong><br>GPT‑5.6 is generally available in Microsoft Foundry, alongside the Asia-Pacific Data Zone, and hosted agents in Foundry Agent Service. See <a href="#gpt-5-6-pricing-for-sol-terra-and-luna">GPT-5.6 pricing</a>.	</div>

<hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity"/>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">AI only creates value when it shows up in real systems—systems that are reliable, observable, and aligned to business outcomes. More than 100,000 organizations are already building on <a href="https://ai.azure.com/home" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Foundry</a>, and companies like <strong>Adobe, Telefónica, and Tata Consultancy Services</strong> are running agents in production today.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">At Microsoft Build, we laid out a simple promise for the agentic era: developers should be able to build an agent where they already work, run it on infrastructure they can trust, and put it in front of the people who need it— without stitching together disconnected platforms. Today, that vision moves from roadmap to reality with three sets of updates <strong>now</strong> <strong>generally available</strong> in Microsoft Foundry:</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>OpenAI’s latest frontier model series</strong>: GPT-5.6 Sol, GPT-5.6 Terra, and GPT-5.6—each tuned to a different workload, available in Standard Global and Standard Data Zones.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Asia-Pacific Data Zone</strong>, giving APAC customers a regional option to run frontier OpenAI models while keeping data processing within the region.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Production agents</strong> in Foundry Agent Service, with <strong>hosted agents</strong>, <strong>toolboxes</strong>, and <strong>publishing to Microsoft 365 Copilot and Microsoft Teams</strong>.</li>
</ul>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Together, these capabilities bring frontier models, production agent runtime, enterprise-grade identity, security, and compliance controls, and distribution across Microsoft 365 into a single platform—helping organizations move from experimentation to production without assembling disconnected tools and services.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="why-foundry-is-the-best-agent-platform">Why Foundry is the best agent platform</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://ai.azure.com/home" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Foundry</a> is Microsoft&#8217;s end-to-end platform for building, running, governing, and distributing AI agents. Foundry brings together the capabilities organizations need to move agents into production across three pillars:</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Build</strong>: Open and flexible across models and frameworks.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Generate</strong>: Connected to enterprise data, tools, and users.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Govern</strong>: Secured, managed, and optimized for long-term value.</li>
</ul>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">These pillars come together in the latest Foundry updates, helping organizations build, run, and scale production agents on a single platform.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-bloginabox-theme-ump-embed wp-block-msx-ump-embed" class="wp-block-bloginabox-theme-ump-embed">
	<div class="wp-block-embed__wrapper">

<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="build-with-any-framework-and-model-on-the-industry-s-end-to-end-ai-platform">Build with any framework and model on the industry’s end-to-end AI platform</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Agent development starts where developers already work—in GitHub Copilot and Microsoft Visual Studio (VS) Code—with the <a href="https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/intelligentapps/overview" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Foundry Toolkit for VS Code</a> and the <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/how-to/develop/use-microsoft-foundry-skill?tabs=vscode%2Cfoundry-skill-alone" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Foundry skill</a> handling deployment to Foundry. Whether teams build with <a href="https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Agent Framework</a>, <a href="https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/how-tos/copilot-sdk/getting-started" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">GitHub Copilot SDK</a> (generally available), or Claude Agent SDK, Foundry is the production destination—and it all starts with the right model.</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="start-with-the-right-model-for-the-right-job">Start with the right model for the right job</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">An agent is only as capable as the model reasoning behind it. Microsoft Foundry gives organizations access to industry-leading frontier, open-source, and task-specific models through a single platform, allowing teams to choose the right model for every workload.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Today, we&#8217;re making OpenAI&#8217;s GPT-5.6 series generally available in Microsoft Foundry Models and Microsoft Foundry Agent Service:</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>GPT-5.6 Sol</strong> delivers the most advanced reasoning capabilities yet, supporting extended reasoning, agentic workflows, and code-focused scenarios, for the most demanding enterprise workloads.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>GPT-5.6 Terra</strong> is a balanced model for everyday work, delivering performance competitive with GPT-5.5 at a lower cost, making it ideal for scaling intelligent applications across the enterprise.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>GPT-5.6 Luna</strong> is the fastest and most affordable model in the family, making it well suited to high-volume, latency-sensitive workloads.</li>
</ul>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Together, the GPT-5.6 series gives organizations the flexibility to match model capability, cost, and performance requirements to specific business scenarios, rather than forcing every workload onto a single model.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Customers consistently tell us that access to new models matters as much as model quality. That’s why we are making <strong>GPT-5.6</strong>—the latest model—available through <strong>Global Standard and Global Priority Processing for all the existing 28 global regions, Data Zones Standard, and Global Provisioned from day one</strong>. This enables customers to adopt the latest frontier AI innovations where they have already built, deployed, and scaled their applications.</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="gpt-5-6-pricing-for-sol-terra-and-luna">GPT-5.6 pricing for Sol, Terra, and Luna</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The following table outlines GPT-5.6 pricing for Sol, Terra, and Luna in Microsoft Foundry. Use this pricing information to compare model options and plan deployment costs:</p>



<figure class="wp-block-table alignleft is-style-regular has-h-5-font-size"><table class="has-fixed-layout"><tbody><tr><td class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center" rowspan="2">Model </td><td class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center" rowspan="2">Deployment </td><td class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center" colspan="2">Pricing (USD $/million tokens) </td></tr><tr><td class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center"><strong>Input</strong> </td><td class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center"><strong>Output</strong> </td></tr><tr><td class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center"><strong>GPT-5.6 Sol</strong> </td><td class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center">Standard Global </td><td class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center">5.00 </td><td class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center">30.00 </td></tr><tr><td class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center"><strong>GPT-5.6 Terra</strong> </td><td class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center">Standard Global </td><td class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center">2.50 </td><td class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center">15.00 </td></tr><tr><td class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center"><strong>GPT-5.6 Luna</strong> </td><td class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center">Standard Global </td><td class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center">1.00 </td><td class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center">6.00 </td></tr></tbody></table></figure>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="run-frontier-ai-where-your-business-operates">Run frontier AI where your business operates</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">More models, in more regions, are only half of what platform expansion means. The other half is more places to run them compliantly. This is exactly what the Asia-Pacific Data Zone delivers. Today we&#8217;re also announcing the <strong>general availability of the Asia-Pacific (APAC) Data Zone for Microsoft Foundry</strong>, enabling APAC customers to run frontier OpenAI models while keeping data processing within the Asia-Pacific regions, with no separate environment to stitch together and no waiting for capability to catch up.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">With Global, Data Zone, and Regional deployment options available in Foundry, organizations can align AI adoption with their sovereignty, compliance, performance, and scale requirements while maintaining a consistent development and operations experience across environments.</p>



<blockquote class="wp-block-quote has-quote-default-font-size is-layout-flow wp-block-quote-is-layout-flow">
<p class="wp-block-paragraph">As financial institutions adopt AI, responsible data handling becomes foundational to trust. Microsoft Foundry’s APAC Data Zone allows us to keep data processing regionally anchored while accessing advanced AI models at scale. This gives us the confidence to accelerate AI innovation responsibly and reinforces our ambition to be a leading AI-powered financial platform in Asia.</p>
<cite>—Hongsoo Kim, Chief Data and AI Officer (CDAO), Viva Republica (Toss)</cite></blockquote>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="generate-impact-with-action-oriented-context-aware-agents">Generate impact with action-oriented, context aware agents</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">A&nbsp;capable model is only the starting point.&nbsp;To put one to work&nbsp;in production,&nbsp;an agent&nbsp;also&nbsp;needs&nbsp;somewhere to run, knowledge of your business, governed access to its tools, memory that carries across interactions&nbsp;and acts on real-world events, and a path to the people who use it.&nbsp;Foundry provides each of&nbsp;these&nbsp;as a&nbsp;built-in&nbsp;capability,&nbsp;designed to&nbsp;work&nbsp;together.</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Where it lives: </strong><a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/agents/concepts/hosted-agents" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>Hosted agents</strong></a><strong>&nbsp;in Foundry Agent Service is now generally available,&nbsp;</strong>giving developers&nbsp;one production runtime for agents built with any framework&nbsp;and harness—Microsoft Agent Framework, GitHub Copilot SDK, LangGraph,&nbsp;OpenClaw,&nbsp;Hermes,&nbsp;and others. It&#8217;s enterprise-ready on day one:&nbsp;<strong>Network&nbsp;isolation&nbsp;with&nbsp;Microsoft Azure Virtual Network (VNet) integration</strong>&nbsp;keeps&nbsp;agent traffic inside your security boundary. For long-running workloads, the new&nbsp;<strong>resilient&nbsp;task support in hosted agents&nbsp;(</strong><a href="https://aka.ms/ResilientAgent/PrPr" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>private&nbsp;preview</strong></a><strong>)</strong>&nbsp;makes&nbsp;it&nbsp;easier to build agents that survive failures. The platform provides primitives to keep the sandbox running,&nbsp;your harness provides checkpointing, and together they allow an&nbsp;agent to resume when it restarts. The result: multi-turn conversations, reasoning loops, and human-in-the-loop approvals can pick up where they left off, without developers having to build their own recovery, retry, and state-management.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>How it talks: </strong><a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/ai-services/speech-service/how-to-voice-live-hosted-agent-integration" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>Hosted agents with Voice Live</strong></a><strong> is now generally available.</strong> Developers can add&nbsp;real-time voice experiences to the agents they built with the frameworks they prefer, using the Azure VoiceLive SDK.&nbsp;</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>What it knows</strong>: Foundry<strong>&nbsp;</strong>gives agents access to enterprise knowledge without&nbsp;requiring&nbsp;developers to&nbsp;build a&nbsp;complex&nbsp;retrieval pipeline from scratch. Microsoft IQ brings together <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-365/copilot/extensibility/work-iq/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>Work IQ</strong></a> for real-time awareness of your Microsoft 365 environment, <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/fabric/iq/overview" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>Fabric IQ</strong></a> for your structured data, and <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/webiq" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>Web IQ</strong></a> for low-latency live web grounding—all unified behind <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/agents/concepts/what-is-foundry-iq?tabs=portal" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>Foundry IQ</strong></a>, now generally available as the SLA-backed knowledge layer behind every Foundry agent.&nbsp;</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>How it reaches its tools: </strong><a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/agents/how-to/tools/toolbox?pivots=python" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>Toolboxes in Foundry</strong></a><strong> is generally available. </strong>Instead of shipping every tool definition on every request, a toolbox dynamically selects the right tool for the job—giving agents governed, curated access while dramatically reducing the token overhead of large tool sets.&nbsp;</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>How it&nbsp;remembers&nbsp;and&nbsp;responds to the world: </strong><a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/agents/concepts/what-is-memory?tabs=conversational-agent" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>Memory</strong></a><strong>&nbsp;and&nbsp;</strong><a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/agents/concepts/routines" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>routines</strong></a><strong>&nbsp;in Foundry Agent Service are in public preview.&nbsp;</strong>Memory (procedural, user, and session) lets agents carry context across interactions. Routines run any agent on a recurring schedule or timer so it acts without a user prompt;&nbsp;new event-based triggers, powered by the connector gateway, now let the same agent wake up the moment an upstream system signals a change—a ticket filed, a file landing in storage, a workflow completing.&nbsp;</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>How it reaches users: </strong><a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/agents/how-to/publish-copilot" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>Publishing to Microsoft Teams and Microsoft 365 Copilot</strong></a><strong> is generally available next week.</strong>&nbsp;The agents your developers build land in the applications where&nbsp;hundreds&nbsp;of millions of people already do their work with identity, permissions, and policy flowing through automatically.&nbsp;And&nbsp;it works even for network-isolated agents: when a project runs behind a private endpoint, you publish through a <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/agents/how-to/publish-copilot-virtual-network?view=foundry" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>documented flow</strong></a> rather than the one-click button. The agent stays on your private network while Microsoft&#8217;s channel adapters reach it through your own firewall and reverse proxy.</li>
</ul>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="govern-and-optimize-the-full-ai-lifecycle-with-observability-and-controls">Govern&nbsp;and&nbsp;optimize&nbsp;the full AI lifecycle with observability and controls</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">An agent you&nbsp;can&#8217;t&nbsp;see,&nbsp;improve, or secure is an agent you&nbsp;can&#8217;t&nbsp;put&nbsp;in production—so Foundry treats trust as a platform priority, not a developer responsibility. What&#8217;s&nbsp;new in this release closes the loop around everything that happens after you&nbsp;build: seeing what an agent did, making it better, and proving it&#8217;s worth running.</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>How you see what it did—</strong><a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/observability/quickstarts/quickstart-tracing-hosted-agent?tabs=azd" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>tracing</strong></a><strong>&nbsp;and&nbsp;</strong><a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/observability/quickstarts/quickstart-evaluate-hosted-agent?tabs=azd" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>evaluation</strong></a><strong>&nbsp;for hosted agents, generally available. </strong>See exactly what an agent did, why, and where it went wrong, and evaluate behavior systematically before and after you ship.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>How it gets better and cheaper</strong>—<a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/agents/quickstarts/quickstart-optimize-hosted-agent" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>agent optimizer in Foundry Agent Service</strong></a>, in public preview. It tests your prompts, skills, models, and tools together and automatically&nbsp;identifies&nbsp;better configurations—often letting you hold quality while moving to a smaller, cheaper model.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>How you prove its value</strong>—<a href="https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/azure-ai-foundry-blog/introducing-roi-for-agents-in-foundry/4531970" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>ROI for agents in Microsoft Foundry</strong></a>, in private preview. It connects an agent&#8217;s traces, business-value evaluations, and operating cost into a single view—surfacing KPIs like net value, total cost, and current ROI in the dashboard, so&nbsp;customers&nbsp;can see whether a production agent is creating more value than it costs to run, and drill into traces when it isn&#8217;t.&nbsp;</li>
</ul>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">As agents scale from pilots to thousands of runs a day, Foundry gives teams the levers to keep spend predictable without leaving the platform.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">That starts with choice.</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Choose how you deploy.</strong> Foundry offers Global, Data Zone, and Regional deployments,&nbsp;so you can align AI to your sovereignty, compliance, and performance requirements, running frontier models while keeping data processing in-region.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Choose how you pay for&nbsp;model&nbsp;inference.</strong> A full spectrum of offers—Standard, Priority Processing, Provisioned Throughput, and Batch—lets you optimize for agility, latency, throughput, and cost on one platform.</li>
</ul>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">On top of that foundation,&nbsp;<a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/openai/concepts/model-router" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"><strong>model&nbsp;router</strong></a> matches each request to the right model, <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/openai/how-to/prompt-caching" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"><strong>prompt&nbsp;caching</strong></a> cuts redundant computation,&nbsp;and <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/openai/how-to/spillover-traffic-management?tabs=portal" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"><strong>PTU&nbsp;spillover</strong></a> and&nbsp;quota optimization preserve service continuity through usage spikes. For agents,&nbsp;<strong>toolboxes in Foundry</strong> send only the tools each request needs,&nbsp;and&nbsp;<strong>agent optimizer</strong>&nbsp;tunes prompts, skills, tools, and model choice against your own evaluators.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">And spend is only half the equation.&nbsp;<strong>ROI for agents in Foundry</strong>&nbsp;connects business value, usage, and cost in one view—so teams can see whether a production agent is creating more value than it costs to run, and where cost is outpacing value.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">For a hands-on walkthrough, watch our new&nbsp;<a href="https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mB0IyELzjRg" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Microsoft Mechanics episode</a>&nbsp;on token economics for agents.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="in-production-what-teams-are-building-on-foundry">In production: What teams are&nbsp;building on Foundry&nbsp;&nbsp;</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The organizations&nbsp;building on&nbsp;Foundry&nbsp;aren&#8217;t&nbsp;experimenting; they&#8217;re&nbsp;shipping, from digital natives to the world&#8217;s largest enterprises.</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Adobe</strong>&nbsp;is building with GitHub, Foundry Agent Service, and Azure Functions, deploying agents&nbsp;for their applications and saving time and work getting to production.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Telefónica</strong>&nbsp;has adopted Microsoft Foundry as the core of their corporate agentic platform, with the first wave of agents tackling network operations—a telco’s most complex, strategic domain—across Microsoft Agent Framework, hosted agents, AI Gateway, and&nbsp;Azure&nbsp;Logic Apps.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Tata Consultancy Services</strong>&nbsp;is using agent optimizer in Foundry Agent Service to improve agent performance with a more structured approach to prompt tuning, helping teams reduce manual effort while measuring gains in task adherence and execution efficiency.</li>
</ul>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The pattern is consistent: teams that once spent weeks integrating, securing, and deploying agents are now doing it in days, on infrastructure that meets their compliance bar, reaching users through tools they already trust.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="get-started">Get started</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Everything&nbsp;in this post is live in&nbsp;<a href="https://ai.azure.com/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Microsoft Foundry</a>.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Follow the <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">documentation</a> and Microsoft Learn courses. Developers can get started in minutes by following the <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/agents/quickstarts/quickstart-hosted-agent?pivots=azd" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Quickstart</a>, which walks through setting up, testing, and deploying a production-ready hosted&nbsp;agent&nbsp;end to end.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Check out <a href="https://github.com/microsoft/ai-agents-for-beginners" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">AI Agents for Beginners</a> for a 12-lesson curriculum, then go deeper with guided labs: <a href="https://microsoftlearning.github.io/mslearn-ai-agents/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Develop AI Agents in Azure</a>, <a href="https://github.com/microsoft/Hosted_Agents_Workshop_dotNET" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Hosted Agents Workshop (.NET)</a>,&nbsp;the&nbsp;<a href="https://github.com/microsoft-foundry/Foundry_Toolkit_for_VSCode_Lab/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Foundry Toolkit for VS Code and hosted agents workshop</a>,&nbsp;and the <a href="https://github.com/microsoft/Learn-Microsoft-Agent-Framework-with-Foundry-ZavaShop-Supply-Chain-Workshop" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">ZavaShop Supply Chain Workshop</a>.&nbsp;To put your agents on a solid quality footing, read <a href="https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/azure-ai-foundry-blog/evaluating-ai-agents-a-practical-guide-with-microsoft-foundry/4500224" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Evaluating AI Agents: A Practical Guide with Microsoft Foundry</a>.&nbsp;</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><strong>Watch:</strong> <a href="https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iR7_57lJOz8" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Foundry Agent Service + Microsoft Agent Framework Explained</a>—Jeff Hollan walks through how to operationalize AI agents from deployment to real-world impact.</p>

										<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<aside class="table-of-contents-block accordion wp-block-bloginabox-theme-table-of-contents" id="accordion-507c5bb2-76b3-42b0-a862-223ebd7f186c" data-bi-aN="table-of-contents">
