<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><em>This blog post is the third part of a blog series called <a href="https://nam06.safelinks.protection.outlook.com/?url=https%3A%2F%2Fazure.microsoft.com%2Fen-us%2Fblog%2Ftag%2Fazure-iaas%2F&amp;data=05%7C02%7Cv-estvirko%40microsoft.com%7C6e1c78a85c2040ce3c1108ded1fb3397%7C72f988bf86f141af91ab2d7cd011db47%7C1%7C0%7C639179074098601042%7CUnknown%7CTWFpbGZsb3d8eyJFbXB0eU1hcGkiOnRydWUsIlYiOiIwLjAuMDAwMCIsIlAiOiJXaW4zMiIsIkFOIjoiTWFpbCIsIldUIjoyfQ%3D%3D%7C0%7C%7C%7C&amp;sdata=mnBTkxRVox%2F%2FXUsLjCQqgid0goRkY3S7s7WFEnDINaI%3D&amp;reserved=0" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure IaaS</a> which will share best practices and guidance to help you build a trusted infrastructure platform—from performance, resiliency, and security to scalability and cost efficiency.</em></p>



<hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity" />



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">As organizations modernize infrastructure, migrate mission-critical workloads, build cloud-native applications, and scale AI—<a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/cloud-cost-optimization-principles-that-still-matter/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">cost efficiency remains a foundational principle</a> of cloud architectures.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://aka.ms/azureIaaS" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Discover the Benefits of Azure IaaS</a></div>
</div>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Yet cloud costs are rarely driven by a single decision. More often, across Azure Infrastructure-as-a-Service (IaaS) environments, they are the result of many compounded architectural choices across compute, storage, and networking. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Common examples include overprovisioning infrastructure when selecting a larger virtual machine than a workload requires or keeping infrequently accessed data on premium storage, building resilient architectures that introduce unnecessary overhead, or collecting and retaining more operational data than is needed. Individually, these decisions may seem minor, but over time they can significantly impact both cost and operational efficiency. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">These challenges become even more important as organizations expand AI initiatives, modernize applications, and support growing performance and resiliency requirements. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  The opportunity lies in addressing these inefficiencies before they become entrenched. By making informed infrastructure decisions during planning, deployment, and ongoing operations, organizations can improve resource utilization, reduce total cost of ownership (TCO), and create a more scalable foundation for future growth.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">In this blog, we&#8217;ll explore some of the most common infrastructure cost challenges organizations face today and examine how Azure IaaS capabilities across compute, storage, and networking can help improve efficiency, reduce TCO, and highlight resources available in the <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/solutions/azure-iaas/#resource-center" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure IaaS Resource Center</a> to help you make more informed decisions.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Many of the most impactful optimization opportunities originate long before a workload enters production. To better understand where these opportunities exist, let&#8217;s examine common efficiency challenges (and solutions) across compute, storage, and networking.
</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="compute-matching-resources-to-workload-requirements"><a id="post-52339-_Toc598210129"></a>Compute: Matching resources to workload requirements</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Compute inefficiencies are often the easiest to identify because they directly affect both performance and infrastructure spend.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  The goal is not simply to select the lowest-cost compute option, but rather to align infrastructure resources with workload requirements while preserving flexibility for future growth.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/virtual-machines">Azure provides a broad portfolio of virtual machine options</a>, enabling organizations to select the architecture, processor type, performance profile, and scale characteristics that best match workload needs; allowing organizations to align infrastructure investments with workload needs rather than paying for unused capacity. 
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Equally important is taking advantage of <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing">Azure&#8217;s flexible pricing options</a>. Depending on workload characteristics, organizations can combine Pay-As-You-Go pricing, <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/offers/savings-plans">Azure savings plans</a>, <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/cost-management-billing/reservations/save-compute-costs-reservations">Azure Reservations</a>, and <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/virtual-machines/spot">Azure Spot Virtual Machines</a> to better align costs with actual usage patterns.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  For highly scalable environments, services such as <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/virtual-machine-scale-sets">Azure Virtual Machine Scale Sets</a> automatically balance compute demand with available capacity by scaling resources up or down in real time, ensuring the environment is right-sized while optimizing cost efficiency. <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/compute-fleet">Azure Compute Fleet</a> help organizations intelligently balance capacity, availability, and price-performance across large deployments; reducing the operational complexity associated with managing infrastructure at scale.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  The result is a compute environment that is not only cost-efficient, but also better aligned to application requirements and business outcomes.
</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="storage-balancing-performance-and-lifecycle-management"><a id="post-52339-_Toc646919671"></a>Storage: Balancing performance and lifecycle management</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Storage inefficiencies often develop gradually, at times making them difficult to identify until environments reach significant scale. The key is to ensure that performance, capacity, and data access requirements remain aligned.
</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="choose-the-right-storage-service-for-the-workload">Choose the right storage service for the workload</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Storage performance requirements vary dramatically across workloads. Some applications demand consistent low-latency block storage, while others prioritize storage capacity, durability, or long-term retention. Selecting the appropriate storage service and performance tier is critical to maximizing both efficiency and value.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  For example:
</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item">Business applications may benefit from <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-gb/products/storage/disks/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Premium SSD v2 offerings</a>.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Business-critical transactional databases may require <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-gb/products/storage/disks/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Ultra Disk</a> to meet stringent low-latency performance requirements.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Large-scale block storage environments can benefit from consolidated architectures using <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/storage/elastic-san">Azure Elastic Storage Area Network (SAN)</a>.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Linux/Windows file shares, home directories, and shared storage scenarios may benefit from <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/storage/files">Azure Files</a> or <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/netapp">Azure NetApp Files</a>.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Object storage workloads often benefit from the alignment between <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/storage/blobs">Azure Blob Storage</a> tiers and data access patterns.</li>
</ul>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="automate-data-lifecycle-management">Automate data lifecycle management</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Equally important is ensuring data remains on the appropriate storage tier throughout its lifecycle. In many environments, data access patterns change significantly over time, yet storage configurations remain static. This disconnect often results in organizations paying for performance they no longer need.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/storage/blobs">Azure Blob Storage</a> provides capabilities that help organizations automatically align storage costs with data access patterns. Automated tiering and lifecycle policies maintain low-latency access for frequently used data while optimizing costs by transitioning infrequently accessed data to lower-cost tiers.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The result is a storage strategy that continuously adapts as usage patterns evolve, without requiring ongoing manual intervention.</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="improve-visibility-across-your-storage-estate">Improve visibility across your storage estate</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Optimization starts with understanding where costs are being generated.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Tools such as <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/storage-discovery">Azure Storage Discovery</a> and <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/storage-actions">Azure Storage Actions</a> can help organizations gain visibility into their storage environments, uncover optimization opportunities, and automate actions across large-scale deployments.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Rather than managing storage account by account, teams can identify patterns and <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/cloud-cost-optimization-how-to-maximize-roi-from-ai-manage-costs-and-unlock-real-business-value/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">implement cost-saving actions</a> consistently across their entire data estate.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Together, these capabilities help organizations move beyond storage provisioning and toward ongoing storage optimization.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="networking-improving-efficiency-without-compromising-resiliency"><a id="post-52339-_Toc155015881"></a>Networking: Improving efficiency without compromising resiliency</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Networking presents a unique optimization challenge because organizations must balance connectivity, performance, resiliency, and operational visibility.</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="achieve-resiliency-more-efficiently">Achieve resiliency more efficiently</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Historically, improving resiliency often requires duplicating infrastructure components, creating additional cost and management overhead. Today, organizations increasingly seek architectures that deliver resiliency while minimizing complexity and excess infrastructure.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/category/networking">Azure networking capabilities</a> help organizations evaluate these tradeoffs more effectively. Services such as <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/expressroute/metro">ExpressRoute Metro</a>, <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/reliability/reliability-nat-gateway">Zone Redundant NAT Gateway</a>, and scalable networking architectures provide opportunities to improve resiliency and scalability while maintaining operational efficiency.
</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="reduce-operational-and-logging-expenses">Reduce operational and logging expenses</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Operational visibility is another important consideration. Network and firewall logs are essential for troubleshooting, security, and governance, but collecting every possible data point can create significant storage and operational costs over time.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Modern filtering and analytics capabilities help teams focus on the most relevant network data, reducing both storage consumption and investigation complexity.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">This gives organizations the information they need while avoiding excessive log growth and long-term retention costs.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">By implementing filtering, automation, and intelligent logging strategies, organizations can focus on the data that provides actionable insights while reducing unnecessary information collection and retention.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="continuous-optimization-is-where-long-term-savings-happen"><a id="post-52339-_Toc1333105540"></a>Continuous optimization is where long-term savings happen</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Infrastructure efficiency is not achieved through a single migration, architecture review, or pricing decision.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">As workloads evolve, usage patterns shift, and new platform capabilities become available, opportunities for optimization continuously emerge.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The organizations that realize the greatest value from cloud investments are often those that treat optimization as an ongoing operational discipline. They regularly evaluate infrastructure utilization, revisit architectural assumptions, automate lifecycle management processes, and adopt new capabilities that improve efficiency across their environments.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">While individual improvements may appear incremental, the cumulative impact can be substantial. A right-sized virtual machine (VM), a more appropriate storage tier, an automated lifecycle policy, or a more efficient networking architecture may each deliver modest savings independently. Together, they create a more efficient, scalable, and resilient infrastructure foundation.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Azure continues to deliver important capabilities such as <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/copilot">Azure Copilot</a> to help customers optimize cloud costs by combining real-time insights, AI-driven recommendations, and automated optimization actions, empowering teams to quickly identify waste, right-size resources, and forecast spend with minimal effort.
</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="continue-your-azure-iaas-optimization-journey"><a id="post-52339-_Toc561313402"></a>Continue your Azure IaaS optimization journey</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Whether you&#8217;re supporting AI workloads, modernizing existing applications, migrating existing workloads, or planning future growth, building efficiency into cloud architectures has never been more important.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The Azure IaaS Resource Center provides guidance, best practices, technical resources, and optimization strategies across compute, storage, and networking to help you design, build, and optimize Azure environments with confidence.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><strong>Visit the </strong><a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/solutions/azure-iaas/#resource-center" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"><strong>Azure IaaS Resource Center</strong></a> to explore cost optimization guidance, architectural best practices, product resources, and tools that can help you maximize value from your Azure infrastructure investments.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">To go deeper, explore the <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/solutions/azure-iaas/#resource-center" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure IaaS Resource Center</a> for tutorials, best practices, and guidance across compute, storage, and networking to help you design and operate resilient infrastructure with greater confidence.</p>

					<description><![CDATA[<p>Claude in Microsoft Foundry is now generally available, hosted on Azure, and running on NVIDIA GB300 Blackwell Ultra, giving teams a faster path from agent experimentation to production.</p>
<p>The post <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/claude-in-microsoft-foundry-is-now-generally-available/">Claude in Microsoft Foundry is now generally available</a> appeared first on <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog">Microsoft Azure Blog</a>.</p>
]]></description>
										<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><strong><em>Claude in Microsoft Foundry is the production path enterprises have been asking for: true frontier model choice, Azure-native controls, simplified procurement, and faster time to value.</em></strong></p>



<hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity" />



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Most enterprise AI projects do not stall because of model quality. They stall because of everything around the model: procurement, governance, networking, and data. Claude in Microsoft Foundry is now generally available, hosted on Azure, giving teams a faster path from agent experimentation to production.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Enterprises can build with Claude through <a href="https://ms.portal.azure.com/auth/login/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">their existing Azure account</a>, using the authentication, billing, networking, governance, and data controls their teams already trust. Instead of solving for infrastructure, teams can focus on building agentic applications that run their work with Claude, in the environment where they already operate.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">This is a real step forward for customers&nbsp;building agentic applications and want to&nbsp;move&nbsp;from AI experimentation to production. <a href="https://claude.com/partners/microsoft-foundry" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Claude brings leading capabilities</a> for coding, agentic workflows, and complex reasoning. Microsoft Foundry brings the enterprise&nbsp;harness&nbsp;to build, evaluate, deploy, and scale those&nbsp;agents&nbsp;on Azure. Together, they give teams a trusted path to&nbsp;production&nbsp;AI with frontier model quality and the Azure controls they already trust.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Today&#8217;s announcement builds on <a href="https://blogs.microsoft.com/blog/2025/11/18/microsoft-nvidia-and-anthropic-announce-strategic-partnerships/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">the strategic partnership Microsoft, NVIDIA, and Anthropic announced in November 2025</a> to expand enterprise access to Claude on NVIDIA accelerated computing. Claude runs on NVIDIA Blackwell Ultra systems, connected by InfiniBand networking, bringing the rack-scale AI infrastructure designed for inference performance and efficiency. </p>



<figure class="wp-block-bloginabox-theme-ump-embed wp-block-msx-ump-embed" class="wp-block-bloginabox-theme-ump-embed">
	<div class="wp-block-embed__wrapper">

<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="build-with-claude-through-your-azure-account"><strong>Build with Claude through your Azure account</strong></h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Developers can access Claude through the Messages API and use core capabilities including prompt caching, extended thinking, and tool streaming. For teams building agents, <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/ai-foundry/agent-service/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Foundry Agent Service</a> uses Claude as the reasoning core to orchestrate multi-step planning, tool use, and task execution across enterprise systems.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Inference is processed in Azure, and customers can choose between Global and US data zones, for teams with data residency requirements. Anthropic operates the inference and is the data processor and SLA provider. Because Claude is available natively through Foundry, teams can work inside the Azure environment they already use. They can authenticate with <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/entra/identity/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Entra ID</a>, apply Azure role-based access controls, manage access through existing governance policies, and track usage through familiar Azure management experiences.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">For high-sensitivity workloads, <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/responsible-ai/openai/data-privacy?tabs=azure-portal" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">zero data retention</a> is also available, so prompts and completions are not retained by Anthropic after the API call completes. For commercial teams, it also simplifies how Claude is purchased and consumed. Claude usage is billed in Claude Consumption Units (CCU), a single, consolidated line on your Azure bill, with MACC drawdown and per-model detail in Foundry unchanged.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">For many enterprises, that matters as much as model capability. The barrier to production isn&#8217;t only whether a model is powerful enough, it&#8217;s whether teams can procure it, govern it, secure it, and operate it at scale inside their existing cloud. With Claude in Foundry, they get frontier capabilities in an Azure environment that aligns with enterprise requirements for security, compliance posture, governance, and data residency.</p>



<blockquote class="wp-block-quote has-quote-default-font-size is-layout-flow wp-block-quote-is-layout-flow">
<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Running Anthropic’s models on Azure has given us the sustained throughput and reliability our enterprise customers expect. The combination of frontier model quality and enterprise-grade infrastructure is what makes Bolt viable for the Fortune 500.</p>
<cite>—Gary Ballabio, Vice President, Partnerships, Bolt</cite></blockquote>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="customers-are-already-building-with-claude-in-foundry"><strong>Customers are already building with Claude&nbsp;in Foundry</strong></h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Enterprises&nbsp;aren’t&nbsp;just running isolated&nbsp;pilots;&nbsp;they’re&nbsp;building production systems&nbsp;and agents&nbsp;that need throughput, reliability, governance, security, and scale.</p>



<blockquote class="wp-block-quote has-quote-default-font-size is-layout-flow wp-block-quote-is-layout-flow">
<p class="wp-block-paragraph">At NVIDIA, we use autonomous AI agents every day to help our teams move faster and think bigger. Anthropic’s Claude models bring strong reasoning, coding and enterprise capabilities that are valuable for complex technical work. With Claude now available in Microsoft Foundry running on NVIDIA GB300 GPUs, more organizations can run advanced, specialized AI agents with the performance, scale and security needed for production.</p>
<cite>—Justin Boitano, Vice President and GM of Enterprise Computing, NVIDIA</cite></blockquote>



<blockquote class="wp-block-quote has-quote-default-font-size is-layout-flow wp-block-quote-is-layout-flow">
<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Our customers describe their tests in plain English, and Momentic runs through the interface to verify everything works before a release ships. We found Claude’s Opus models especially suited to this, and running them on Microsoft Foundry we now serve millions of tokens per minute with the reliability our customers depend on.</p>
<cite>—Jeff An, Co-Founder and CEO, Momentic</cite></blockquote>

<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="built-for-coding-agents-and-complex-reasoning"><strong>Built for coding, agents, and complex reasoning</strong></h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://ai.azure.com/catalog/publishers/anthropic" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Claude models</a> are especially well-suited to some of the fastest-growing enterprise AI workloads. For software teams, Claude supports code generation, refactoring, debugging, test creation, and large-scale development workflows. For teams building agents, it powers multi-step reasoning, tool use, planning, and task execution. For business teams, it supports document-heavy analysis, research synthesis, and complex decision support.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">In Microsoft Foundry, these capabilities connect to the broader <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure ecosystem</a>. With Foundry Agent Service, teams orchestrate multi-step, goal-driven agents that use Claude as their reasoning core, planning, calling tools, and executing tasks across enterprise systems. Features like <strong><a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/openai/concepts/model-router" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">model router</a></strong> enable customers to automatically route queries to the most appropriate Claude model, <strong>saving up to 50% while improving user satisfaction</strong>. All this governed and monitored by <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/control-plane/overview" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Foundry Control Plane</a> which continuously runs evaluations to ensure agent responses match customer expectations, even blocking responses that violate rules before they reach users.</p>



<blockquote class="wp-block-quote has-quote-default-font-size is-layout-flow wp-block-quote-is-layout-flow">
<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Between Anthropic and Azure, we get the best capabilities in the world and we get the best security in the world. And that’s exactly what nuclear needs. It’s how we compressed a safety analysis that would have taken 200 human days into a single day.</p>
<cite>—Matt Huang, Founding Product Lead, Everstar</cite></blockquote>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">And with <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/ai/microsoft-iq" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft IQ</a>, agents have access to live enterprise context which radically improves value per token, and helps Foundry amplify the impact customers can have: tools like <strong>agent optimizer in Foundry Agent Service</strong> tune the prompts which define agents so they perform better regardless of what model is under the hood.</p>

<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="a-stronger-foundation-for-enterprise-ai-on-azure"><strong>A stronger foundation for enterprise AI on Azure</strong></h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The next phase of enterprise AI will be defined by production systems: coding agents, business process agents, research assistants, customer-facing applications, and domain-specific workflows that operate reliably at scale. That takes more than access to a model. It takes a platform.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">With <a href="https://aka.ms/ClaudeGAdocumentation" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Claude now generally available in Microsoft Foundry</a> and hosted on Azure, customers can build with Anthropic’s leading models, orchestrating them as agents with Foundry Agent Service and grounding them in enterprise knowledge with Microsoft IQ, while using the Azure controls, commitments, and infrastructure they already trust.</p>

		<title>The 2026 Agent Confidence Index: Where 300 builders see real momentum</title>
		<link>https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-cloud/blog/2026/06/29/the-2026-agent-confidence-index-where-300-builders-see-real-momentum/</link>
					<comments>https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-cloud/blog/2026/06/29/the-2026-agent-confidence-index-where-300-builders-see-real-momentum/#respond</comments>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Amanda Silver]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Mon, 29 Jun 2026 15:15:00 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[AI + machine learning]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[AI]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Microsoft Fabric]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Microsoft IQ]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/?p=52418</guid>

					<description><![CDATA[<p>At Microsoft, building trustworthy AI agents is as critical as building powerful ones. New research from the 2026 Agent Confidence Index shows where teams trust agents today—and why human judgment remains the defining skill in the age of AI.</p>
<p>The post <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-cloud/blog/2026/06/29/the-2026-agent-confidence-index-where-300-builders-see-real-momentum/">The 2026 Agent Confidence Index: Where 300 builders see real momentum</a> appeared first on <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog">Microsoft Azure Blog</a>.</p>
]]></description>
										<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<p class="wp-block-paragraph">A couple of months ago, I sat across from my nine-year-old daughter&#8217;s teachers at a parent-teacher conference. They were kind but concerned. She takes her time on assignments, they said—often deep in thought. How would she do on timed tests next year? I told them I&nbsp;wasn&#8217;t&nbsp;worried. What they described as a problem is, to me, one of the most important things she can learn: the ability to take a hard problem and reason through it from beginning to end. In a world optimized&nbsp;for efficiency, qualities like patience, perseverance, and attention to detail are not deficiencies. They are the foundation of sound judgment, which will&nbsp;become the skills we need most.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The more time I spend working with AI, the more convinced I become: the question that matters for her future&nbsp;isn&#8217;t&nbsp;how quickly she can answer. It&#8217;s whether she has the judgment to know when an answer can be trusted.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">I’ve&nbsp;spent decades at Microsoft watching this tension play out: first building tools for other developers, then working&nbsp;across AI as models moved from research curiosities to&nbsp;systems&nbsp;deployed&nbsp;at scale. Now we&#8217;re <a href="https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/same-mission-new-chapter-amanda-k-silver-nsgfc/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">building Microsoft IQ</a>, where we’re&nbsp;exploring&nbsp;how&nbsp;an organization’s collective intelligence can become <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/ai/microsoft-iq?msockid=06bb933471b660601b26879d700c61c2" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">its greatest advantage</a>. Through every one of those chapters, one thing has remained true: it&#8217;s&nbsp;never enough for a system to be&nbsp;powerful,&nbsp;it&nbsp;must also be trustworthy.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Trust is what turns assistance into delegation.&nbsp;When we can trust an agent to do what we intend, within the limits we set,&nbsp;we can hand off the work we never wanted to spend our lives&nbsp;on: the repetitive tasks that drain attention, the mundane work&nbsp;that fills&nbsp;a day without moving anything meaningful forward, the dangerous work humans should not have to do, the work too vast for any individual or team. Agents should take on that toil, extend our reach, and give us back our time for the work that calls for something only&nbsp;humans&nbsp;bring.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">My daughter&nbsp;doesn&#8217;t&nbsp;know any of this yet. But by the time she&#8217;s grown, most&nbsp;of the work that rewards speed and repetition will be work we delegate.&nbsp;What will matter&nbsp;then is exactly what gave her teachers pause: the&nbsp;patience&nbsp;to stay with a hard problem, reason through it, and decide&nbsp;when&nbsp;she’s&nbsp;reached&nbsp;a conclusion she can trust. The very thing&nbsp;they&nbsp;feared&nbsp;might&nbsp;hold her back&nbsp;could be&nbsp;exactly what the next era prizes most.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">So no,&nbsp;I&#8217;m&nbsp;not worried about the timed test. I&nbsp;hope&nbsp;she grows up in a world where software carries the&nbsp;toil&nbsp;and people are freed&nbsp;for&nbsp;the work that is unmistakably ours—to think, to&nbsp;judge,&nbsp;to&nbsp;create,&nbsp;to&nbsp;care for one another. That is the future I want agents to make&nbsp;real.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">But&nbsp;my&nbsp;hope is not&nbsp;evidence&nbsp;it will happen. The future I just described turns&nbsp;on&nbsp;a single question: can we trust agents to do the work? Trust is earned one task at a time. So, I went looking for&nbsp;evidence&nbsp;of where it&#8217;s been earned, and where it hasn&#8217;t.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">For the past year, the conversation around AI agents has circled the same promise:&nbsp;eliminate&nbsp;toil so people can focus on what matters. But I&nbsp;keep coming back to sharper questions.&nbsp;What,&nbsp;exactly,&nbsp;is toilsome? Where does&nbsp;toil&nbsp;actually live in people’s work? What are the technical leaders closest to this shift willing to hand&nbsp;off—and what gives them the confidence to do it? To find out, we <a href="https://www.technologyreview.com/2026/06/29/1139635/agent-confidence-on-the-technical-frontier/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">partnered with MIT Technology Review Insights</a>&nbsp;on new research that draws directly from the people building this frontier. Not the people talking about it,&nbsp;the people doing it. We surveyed 300 technical experts across AI, data, and cloud domains, spanning 12 industries and 4 regions of the world, asking them to rank their confidence across 101 of the top tasks. What we got back is&nbsp;the <a href="https://aka.ms/MITinsightreport" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">2026 Agent Confidence Index</a>,&nbsp;an honest map of where agents are delivering real value, so our community can see&nbsp;what’s&nbsp;working and move forward together with conviction.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://aka.ms/agentconfidenceindex" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer noreferrer noopener">Explore the 2026 Agent Confidence Index report</a></div>
</div>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="learn-from-where-confidence-is-highest"><strong>Learn from where confidence is highest</strong></h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Across the 101 tasks measured, average confidence already lands at&nbsp;<strong>64 out of 100</strong>, and thirty tasks clear 70. The highest scores cluster on work that is both predictable and draining: the late nights, the interruptions, the low-value repetition. Automated report generation leads at 83.5. Boilerplate code generation for new features sits at 82.5, the hours a developer no longer spends rewriting the same patterns, freed for the work that challenges them. Certificate expiration monitoring and renewal, at 81.5, ends the scramble that pulls engineers off high-stakes problems for something entirely routine. Real-time data stream monitoring follows at 80.5, and release note generation from commit history at 79.5, the manual end-of-sprint commit review, gone. This is where frontier teams are already delegating to agents, regularly.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The pattern holds across every discipline. In developer and AI workflows it extends to API client maintenance and code identification; in cloud operations, to ticket routing and cost optimization; in data, to anomaly detection. Wherever it sits in the stack, this is work technical teams now trust agents to own.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">What matters most here isn&#8217;t what the data says about the tasks, it&#8217;s what it says about the people delegating them. When technical experts believe in something deeply enough to hand it real work, that belief ripples outward. It becomes the recommendation they make to their leadership, the solution they build for their customers, and the culture they create for their teams.</p>



<figure data-wp-context="{&quot;imageId&quot;:&quot;6a64da7375aae&quot;}" data-wp-interactive="core/image" data-wp-key="6a64da7375aae" class="wp-block-image aligncenter wp-lightbox-container"><img decoding="async" data-wp-class--hide="state.isContentHidden" data-wp-class--show="state.isContentVisible" data-wp-init="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on--click="actions.showLightbox" data-wp-on--load="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on-window--resize="callbacks.setButtonStyles" src="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-cloud/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/06/word-image-15364-2-2.png" alt="Chart showing trending AI agent tasks across AI, data, and cloud workflows. Highest-confidence use cases include automated report generation and distribution (84), certificate expiration monitoring and renewal (81.5), and boilerplate code generation for new features (82.5). Scores indicate strong confidence in automation for reporting, monitoring, code generation, and cloud operations tasks." class="wp-image-15378" /><button
			class="lightbox-trigger"
			type="button"
			aria-haspopup="dialog"
			aria-label="Enlarge"

				<path fill="#fff" d="M2 0a2 2 0 0 0-2 2v2h1.5V2a.5.5 0 0 1 .5-.5h2V0H2Zm2 10.5H2a.5.5 0 0 1-.5-.5V8H0v2a2 2 0 0 0 2 2h2v-1.5ZM8 12v-1.5h2a.5.5 0 0 0 .5-.5V8H12v2a2 2 0 0 1-2 2H8Zm2-12a2 2 0 0 1 2 2v2h-1.5V2a.5.5 0 0 0-.5-.5H8V0h2Z" />
			</svg>
		</button></figure>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="even-the-toughest-agent-tasks-are-gaining-traction"><strong>Even the toughest agent tasks are gaining traction</strong></h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Here&#8217;s what strikes me most: the tasks ranked lower on <a href="https://aka.ms/agentconfidenceindex">the index</a> are still high in absolute terms. Service mesh configuration and troubleshooting sits at 37.5, database schema migration scripting at 46.5, memory leak detection at 48.5. These sit at the very frontier, the interconnected, high-stakes work where investment and innovation are concentrated right now.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Consider what they demand. Service mesh configuration touches many systems at once. Database migration carries real stakes, requiring precision across data, application, and infrastructure layers at the same time. Memory leak detection means diving deep into a system&#8217;s behavior under load, accounting for conditions that shift from one deployment to the next. These are the challenges that have separated great engineers from exceptional ones—and even here, experts see agents helping. Not carrying the work alone, but contributing where it used to be unthinkable. That confidence is still climbing, and that&#8217;s telling.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">We&#8217;re shipping new capabilities constantly to support this momentum. Database migration tooling in <a href="https://github.com/features/ai/github-app">GitHub Copilot</a> now covers not just scripts but the full application and infrastructure migration story. The <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/sre-agent">Azure Site Reliability Engineering (SRE) Agent</a> brings decades of experience operating Azure at scale and deep profiling capabilities directly into memory analysis and performance diagnosis.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="why-human-judgment-remains-paramount"><strong>Why human judgment remains paramount</strong></h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">When we asked technical experts how they&#8217;re navigating agent adoption, <strong>59% named &#8220;keeping humans in the loop&#8221; as their top priority</strong>—ahead of better observability, ahead of governance documentation, and ahead of everything else. That&#8217;s a mark of maturity. Teams moving forward with clarity treat agent oversight as non-negotiable, regardless of how capabilities evolve.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The boundary itself is straightforward. Agents excel at well-specified, high-volume, reversible work: they synthesize data, automate known workflows, and surface anomalies at a speed and scale no human team could match. The moment a decision becomes high-stakes, context-dependent, or hard to undo, a human signs off. That isn&#8217;t a limitation of the technology, it&#8217;s the architecture of a trustworthy system.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">What&#8217;s changing, and what remains underappreciated, is the skill it takes to draw that boundary well: the discipline of <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/how-to/evaluate-generative-ai-app">full-lifecycle evaluations</a> and <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/guardrails/guardrails-overview">guardrails</a>. Success means measuring agent output against intent and keeping behavior inside your business strategy. It&#8217;s new territory for most engineering teams, and it&#8217;s becoming table stakes for modern software faster than most organizations realize. The good news: the same tools generating the work can help you build the harness. <a href="https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/concepts/agents/github-copilot-app">Ask GitHub Copilot</a> to write the evals and it will. Frontier teams are already doing this, and it&#8217;s why they&#8217;re pulling ahead.</p>


<figure data-wp-context="{&quot;imageId&quot;:&quot;6a64da7376bf9&quot;}" data-wp-interactive="core/image" data-wp-key="6a64da7376bf9" class="wp-block-image aligncenter wp-lightbox-container"><img decoding="async" data-wp-class--hide="state.isContentHidden" data-wp-class--show="state.isContentVisible" data-wp-init="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on--click="actions.showLightbox" data-wp-on--load="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on-window--resize="callbacks.setButtonStyles" src="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-cloud/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/06/word-image-15364-3-2.webp" alt="MIT Technology Review Insights survey chart titled &ldquo;Concerns are addressed by keeping humans in the loop and increasing observability.&rdquo; Respondents most frequently identified keeping humans in the loop (59%) and increasing observability/monitoring and tracing (53%) as key ways to address concerns around AI agents. <h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="agents-are-opening-career-doors-for-engineering">Agents are opening career doors for engineering</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Across system reliability and site operations, evaluations and quality assurance, and data pipeline management, <strong>80% or more of respondents see meaningful career opportunity ahead</strong>. We believe this is one of the most significant moments in the history of building software, not because agents replace what technical people do, but because what&#8217;s left when they take on the toil is the work that defines a career: the judgment calls, the architectural vision, the reasoning to navigate complexity under pressure. That fluency will define the next generation of technical leadership.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">We&#8217;re living this shift at Microsoft, right alongside our customers. Junior developers are using agents to explore codebases on their own and arriving at mentoring conversations with sharper, more sophisticated questions. Senior engineers are covering more ground because the repetitive work that used to fill their days is now delegated, and the work that&#8217;s left is harder, more interesting, and more consequential. Both are growing into more capable versions of themselves. For me, that&#8217;s the outcome I&#8217;ve always believed technology could deliver.</p>



<figure data-wp-context="{&quot;imageId&quot;:&quot;6a64da737e4ee&quot;}" data-wp-interactive="core/image" data-wp-key="6a64da737e4ee" class="wp-block-image aligncenter wp-lightbox-container"><img decoding="async" data-wp-class--hide="state.isContentHidden" data-wp-class--show="state.isContentVisible" data-wp-init="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on--click="actions.showLightbox" data-wp-on--load="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on-window--resize="callbacks.setButtonStyles" src="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-cloud/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/06/word-image-15364-4-2.png" alt="MIT Technology Review Insights survey chart showing confidence that AI agents will enhance careers across technology disciplines. <h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="an-integrated-approach-to-intelligence-and-trust"><strong>An integrated approach to intelligence and trust</strong></h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Designing more sophisticated agent systems has made one thing clear: agents thrive in well-integrated environments, working best when your whole stack draws on a single source of truth. The high-confidence tasks are the ones we&#8217;ve already figured out; the meaningful frontier is the harder, interconnected work,  and that&#8217;s exactly where observability, governance, security, and unified intelligence have to operate as one.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/ai/microsoft-iq" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft IQ</a> brings your <strong>enterprise context into a</strong> <strong>single, continuous intelligence layer</strong>. Within it, <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-365/copilot/extensibility/work-iq/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Work IQ</a> builds semantic understanding of how your business operates across email, calendar, meetings, chats, files, people, and collaboration patterns. Such depth of knowledge is the reason technical teams choose us and it’s what drives my focus and passion in learning how people actually work so their agents get them. My colleague Kim Manis, CVP of Product for Microsoft Fabric, has <a href="https://aka.ms/Confidence-Index-Data-Blog" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">written specifically about what this means for data professionals</a>, and the integral role of <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-fabric/features/iq" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Fabric IQ</a>.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">It’s all part of the <a href="https://blogs.microsoft.com/blog/2026/06/02/ai-alone-wont-change-your-business-the-system-running-it-will/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Agent Platform</a>, which is becoming <strong>the operating system for enterprise AI at scale</strong>. From building in <a href="https://github.com/features/copilot" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">GitHub</a> and contextualizing with <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/ai/microsoft-iq?msockid=06bb933471b660601b26879d700c61c2" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft IQ</a>, to running in <a href="https://devblogs.microsoft.com/foundry/agent-service-build2026/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Foundry</a> and governing in <a href="https://aka.ms/BUILD_SecurityBlog" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Agent 365</a>, Microsoft is uniquely positioned to help customers bring together data, models, agents, and human judgment into a continuously improving and secure system.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Frontier transformation is being led by builders like you.</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="next-steps"><strong>Next steps:</strong></h3>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item">Download <a href="https://aka.ms/MITinsightreport" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">The 2026 Agent Confidence Index</a> from our partners at MIT Technology Review Insights.It is a free, ungated deep dive into all 101 tasks, broken out by role and workflow, with the patterns and reasoning behind where confidence is strongest and the frontier is expanding.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Join us at the <a href="https://www.ai.engineer/worldsfair/2026" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">AI Engineering World’s Fair</a> (June 29-July 2) where our very own <a href="https://www.linkedin.com/in/pabloc/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Pablo Castro</a> will keynote, and our teams will offer <a href="https://www.ai.engineer/worldsfair/schedule?q=Microsoft&amp;view=list" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">16 breakout sessions and 4 labs</a>. Swing by the Microsoft booth as well to explore an interactive 3D visualization of the Index data. We want to hear what’s working for you right now.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Learn more about <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/ai/microsoft-iq" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Microsoft IQ</a> and how it connects across <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-365/copilot/extensibility/work-iq/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Work IQ</a>, <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-fabric/features/IQ" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Fabirc IQ</a>, <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/agents/concepts/what-is-foundry-iq?tabs=portal" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Foundry IQ</a>, and the newly announced <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/webiq?msockid=06bb933471b660601b26879d700c61c2" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Web IQ</a>. You can catch up on all the developer innovation from Microsoft Build through <a href="https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=FFMm454fxNA" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Satya Nadella’s keynote</a>, <a href="https://blogs.microsoft.com/blog/2026/06/02/microsoft-build-2026-be-yourself-at-work/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Kyle Daigle’s blog post</a>, and the <a href="https://github.com/microsoft/Build-CLI" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Build CLI</a>.</li>
